class Solution:
    def canThreePartsEqualSum(self, arr: List[int]) -> bool:
        total = sum(arr)
            each_sum = total//3

                count += 1
            if count == 2:

        return False

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- staying in diagnosis mode for too long without testing the next plausible edit;
- moving from a local repair into a fresh branch of speculative causes when the current path has not been rechecked yet;
- turning a focused fix into a broad rewrite unless the surrounding code clearly forces it.

    def canThreePartsEqualSum(self, arr: List[int]) -> bool:
        total = sum(arr)
        if total % 3 != 0:
            return False

                cur = 0
            if parts == 2:

        return False

Let's craft final:

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def canThreePartsEqualSum(self, arr: List[int]) -> bool:
        total = sum(arr)
        if total % 3 != 0:
            return False

        target = total // 3
        current = 0
        parts = 0
        n = len(arr)

        for i, x in enumerate(arr):
            current += x
            if current == target:
                current = 0
                parts += 1

            if parts == 2:
                return i < n - 1

        return False

Need check if `parts == 2` after increment at i; if i < n-1 true. But what if parts becomes 2 at i and i < n-1, but the second part is actually the last element? i < n-1 false if last. Good.

Potential subtlety: If target=0 and current==target at i=0, parts=1. Then if i=1 and current==target, parts=2. If n=2, i<1 false. Good. If n=3, i<2 true. Good.

Potential subtlety: If arr = [0, 0, 0, 0], n=4. i=0 parts1; i=1 parts2; i<3 true -> true. Partition [0], [0], [0,0]. Valid. If arr = [0, 0, 0], i=0 parts1; i=1 parts2; i<2 true -> true. Partition [0], [0], [0]. Valid. If arr = [0, 0], false. Good.

Let's decide final code exactly:

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def canThreePartsEqualSum(self, arr: List[int]) -> bool:
        total = sum(arr)
        if total % 3 != 0:
            return False

        target = total // 3
        current = 0
        parts = 0
        n = len(arr)

        for i, x in enumerate(arr):
            current += x
            if current == target:
                current = 0
                parts += 1

            if parts == 2:
                return i < n - 1

        return False

=== RAW ANSWER ===
.</think>```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def canThreePartsEqualSum(self, arr: List[int]) -> bool:
        total = sum(arr)
        if total % 3 != 0:
            return False

        target = total // 3
        current = 0
        parts = 0
        n = len(arr)

        for i, x in enumerate(arr):
            current += x
            if current == target:
                current = 0
                parts += 1

            if parts == 2:
                return i < n - 1

        return False
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def canThreePartsEqualSum(self, arr: List[int]) -> bool:
        total = sum(arr)
        if total % 3 != 0:
            return False

        target = total // 3
        current = 0
        parts = 0
        n = len(arr)

        for i, x in enumerate(arr):
            current += x
            if current == target:
                current = 0
                parts += 1

            if parts == 2:
                return i < n - 1

        return False
